<section class="section" id="subsystem-theme">
  <div class="section-head"><h2>Themes</h2></div>
  <div class="cheat">
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/blueprint">theme / blueprint</a> <span class="about">White lines on the deep blue of a cyanotype drawing: a faint grid on the page, a fine one every 20 pixels and a stronger one every 100 as on drawing paper, the headings lettered in capitals as a title block is, the links the yellow of an engineer&#039;s marking pencil - for technical pages, demos and diagrams.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;blueprint&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the blueprint theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/contrast">theme / contrast</a> <span class="about">Black on white, a dark blue for the links: every text of the frame at 7 to 1 or more, WCAG AAA, the links underlined and the focus ringed thick, so colour is never all that tells them apart.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;contrast&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the contrast theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/contrast-dark">theme / contrast-dark</a> <span class="about">White on black, yellow for the links, as the high-contrast black scheme of an operating system has them: the dark counterpart of contrast, every text of the frame at 7 to 1 or more, WCAG AAA, the links underlined, the focus ringed thick in yellow and the lines light enough to be seen.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;contrast-dark&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the contrast-dark theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/dark">theme / dark</a> <span class="about">The frame&#039;s own dark colours, kept dark whatever the system or the frame&#039;s switch says, the side&#039;s headings lightened to WCAG AA.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;dark&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the dark theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/forest">theme / forest</a> <span class="about">A light page in the greens of leaves, a deep forest-green side and accent.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;forest&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the forest theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/light">theme / light</a> <span class="about">The frame&#039;s own light colours, kept light whatever the system or the frame&#039;s switch says, with the two greys and the soft accent the frame&#039;s set has below WCAG AA - its dim text, the side&#039;s headings, a link on the accent&#039;s tint - darkened to it.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;light&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the light theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/mono">theme / mono</a> <span class="about">Greys only, black links underlined and black buttons: a page whose colour is all in its pictures and charts.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;mono&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the mono theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/newspaper">theme / newspaper</a> <span class="about">Black on newsprint, a deep red for the links and a serif for every line, the headings set tight as a paper&#039;s are.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;newspaper&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the newspaper theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/night">theme / night</a> <span class="about">A dark page for reading late, in warm colours alone: parchment text on a brown-black ground, an amber accent, the messages in olive, gold and coral - every colour&#039;s blue below its red and its green, as a screen&#039;s night mode shifts away from blue light.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;night&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the night theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/ocean">theme / ocean</a> <span class="about">Deep navy water under a pale blue text, the links the cyan of a shallow lagoon.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;ocean&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the ocean theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/paper">theme / paper</a> <span class="about">Warm off-white paper and soft black ink, a blue-black accent like a fountain pen&#039;s, the type a book face: quiet pages for reading.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;paper&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the paper theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/projection">theme / projection</a> <span class="about">Large readable type and strong focus/state boundaries for projected pages.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;projection&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the projection theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/readable">theme / readable</a> <span class="about">A page set for readers with dyslexia, after the British Dyslexia Association&#039;s Dyslexia Style Guide: dark text on cream rather than black on white, a plain, evenly spaced sans-serif (Atkinson Hyperlegible where it is installed, else the guide&#039;s Verdana, Tahoma or Trebuchet), 16 pixels, the lines 1.6 apart, the letters .06em and the words .16em further apart - within what WCAG 1.4.12 asks a page to bear - paragraphs no wider than 70 characters, bold instead of italics, no block capitals in the side&#039;s headings, and the messages in Okabe and Ito&#039;s blue, orange and vermilion, darkened to AA: no green and no red, which the guide asks to avoid for colour-blind readers.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;readable&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the readable theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/sepia">theme / sepia</a> <span class="about">The browns of an old photograph, dark umber on a parchment page and a saddle-brown accent: easy on the eyes for a long read.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;sepia&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the sepia theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/solarized-dark">theme / solarized-dark</a> <span class="about">The deep blue-green and cream of Solarized&#039;s dark mode: its base tones for the page, the panels, the side and the code, its blue for the links, its green, yellow and red for the messages.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;solarized-dark&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the solarized-dark theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/solarized-light">theme / solarized-light</a> <span class="about">The cream and blue-green of Solarized&#039;s light mode: its base tones for the page, the side and the code, its blue for the links, its green, yellow and red for the messages.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;solarized-light&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the solarized-light theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/theme/terminal">theme / terminal</a> <span class="about">Green phosphor on black with an amber accent, every letter monospaced, as an old video terminal showed it.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:theme</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;terminal&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span><span class="hl-htm">&lt;p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span>A box in the terminal theme.<span class="hl-htm">&lt;/p</span><span class="hl-htm">&gt;</span><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">/pad:theme</span><span class="hl-brc">}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
  </div>
</section>

<section class="section" id="subsystem-palette">
  <div class="section-head"><h2>Colour palettes</h2></div>
  <div class="cheat">
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/amber">palette / amber</a> <span class="about">Warm scale from cream to dark amber.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;amber&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/batlow">palette / batlow</a> <span class="about">Fabio Crameri&#039;s sequential scale batlow, from a dark navy through teal, a moss green, olive and ochre to salmon and a pale pink: perceptually uniform, its lightness rising all the way, so it prints as a ramp in grey, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;batlow&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/blue-orange">palette / blue-orange</a> <span class="about">Deviations around a neutral #f2f2f2 midpoint.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;blue-orange&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/blues">palette / blues</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s single-hue sequential scale of nine blues, from nearly white to navy (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;blues&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/brbg">palette / brbg</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale of eleven classes from brown through a light grey to blue-green, readable by colour-blind eyes (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;brbg&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/cividis">palette / cividis</a> <span class="about">A perceptually uniform scale from navy to yellow made to look alike to people with and without a red-green colour vision deficiency (Nu&ntilde;ez, Anderton and Renslow, 2018).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;cividis&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/dark2">palette / dark2</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s qualitative Dark2, eight deep colours to tell categories apart - teal green, orange, slate violet, magenta, green, mustard, brown and grey (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): darker and more saturated than its pastels, so thin lines and small marks stay visible on a light surface; ColorBrewer marks its first three safe for colour-blind eyes, four to six possibly, more not.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;dark2&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/earth">palette / earth</a> <span class="about">Eight tones of soil, clay, moss and sand, PAD&#039;s own qualitative palette: umber, olive, ochre, slate, sienna, moss, sand and a dark brown - quiet colours for a page in warm tones.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;earth&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/glasbey">palette / glasbey</a> <span class="about">Thirty-two colours to tell many categories apart, each chosen as far as it can be from all before it, by the method of Chris Glasbey, Gerie van der Heijden, Vivian Toh and others (&quot;Colour displays for categorical images&quot;, Color Research and Application 32.4, 2007): started from white and black, so none is near either, and no grey among them, every colour of a chroma of 20 or more.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;glasbey&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/greens">palette / greens</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s single-hue sequential scale of nine greens, from nearly white to a deep forest green (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;greens&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/greys">palette / greys</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s sequential scale of nine greys, from white to black (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;greys&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/inferno">palette / inferno</a> <span class="about">A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple, red and orange to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;inferno&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/magma">palette / magma</a> <span class="about">A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple and red to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;magma&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/okabe-ito">palette / okabe-ito</a> <span class="about">The eight colours Masataka Okabe and Kei Ito chose to stay apart for every kind of colour vision (Color Universal Design, 2008): orange, sky blue, bluish green, yellow, blue, vermillion, reddish purple and black - black put last here, where the original lists it first, as it vanishes on a dark surface.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;okabe-ito&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/oranges">palette / oranges</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s single-hue sequential scale of nine oranges, from cream to a dark burnt orange (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;oranges&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/paired">palette / paired</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s qualitative Paired, twelve colours in six pairs, a light and a dark of one hue each - blue, green, red, orange, purple, and a pale yellow with a brown (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): for categories that come in pairs - last year and this, planned and done - the pair&#039;s hue telling the group and its lightness the member; ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes up to four colours, possibly to eight, more not.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;paired&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/pastel">palette / pastel</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s Pastel1, nine light qualitative colours for large areas behind dark text - a background, a map, the fills of a treemap (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;pastel&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/phase">palette / phase</a> <span class="about">Cyclic angle scale; samples stop before the repeated first color.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;phase&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/piyg">palette / piyg</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale of eleven classes from pink through a light grey to yellow-green, readable by colour-blind eyes (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;piyg&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/plasma">palette / plasma</a> <span class="about">A perceptually uniform scale from deep blue through magenta and orange to yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;plasma&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/puor">palette / puor</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale PuOr of eleven classes, from a dark brown through orange to a light grey middle and on through lilac to a dark purple (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), in ColorBrewer&#039;s order, its orange end first: orange against purple, two hues red-green colour-blind eyes still tell apart, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for them.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;puor&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/rdbu">palette / rdbu</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale of eleven classes from dark red through a light grey to dark blue, for values on both sides of a middle - colder and warmer, less and more (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;rdbu&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/rdylbu">palette / rdylbu</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale RdYlBu of eleven classes, from a dark red through orange to a pale yellow middle and on through light blue to a dark indigo (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), for values on both sides of a middle - hotter and colder, above and below: the spread of a rainbow, but ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes at every number of classes, where its Spectral and RdYlGn are at most possibly so, and only up to five.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;rdylbu&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/romao">palette / romao</a> <span class="about">Fabio Crameri&#039;s cyclic scale romaO, for what goes round - an angle, a direction, the hour, the month: from a dark plum through brick, ochre and a pale yellow, a pale green, sky blue and indigo back to the plum, its last colour next to its first.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;romao&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/tol-bright">palette / tol-bright</a> <span class="about">Paul Tol&#039;s bright qualitative scheme of seven colours, told apart by colour-blind eyes and in print: blue, red, green, yellow, cyan, purple and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;tol-bright&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/tol-muted">palette / tol-muted</a> <span class="about">Paul Tol&#039;s muted qualitative scheme of ten colours, softer than the bright one and still told apart by colour-blind eyes: rose, indigo, sand, green, cyan, wine, teal, olive, purple and a pale grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;tol-muted&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/tol-vibrant">palette / tol-vibrant</a> <span class="about">Paul Tol&#039;s vibrant qualitative scheme of seven colours, made for TensorBoard and told apart by colour-blind eyes: orange, blue, cyan, magenta, red, teal and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;tol-vibrant&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/turbo">palette / turbo</a> <span class="about">Google&#039;s rainbow made smooth, by Anton Mikhailov (2019): from a dark indigo through blue, cyan, green, yellow and orange to a dark red, every step a visible change of hue, for a depth map or a field where the eye must read a value at a glance.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;turbo&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/vik">palette / vik</a> <span class="about">Fabio Crameri&#039;s diverging scale vik, from a dark navy through blue and a pale blue to a light middle and on through tan and rust to a dark red-brown, for values on both sides of a middle: perceptually uniform, its two halves nearly alike in lightness, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;vik&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/viridis">palette / viridis</a> <span class="about">A perceptually uniform scale from dark purple through blue and green to yellow, the default of matplotlib (St&eacute;fan van der Walt and Nathaniel Smith, 2015): even steps look even, it prints in grey as a ramp, and the common forms of colour blindness read it.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;viridis&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/ylgnbu">palette / ylgnbu</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s multi-hue sequential scale YlGnBu of nine classes, from a pale yellow through green and teal to blue and a dark navy (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): its hue turns as its lightness falls, so the steps of a map or a heatmap tell apart better than in one hue, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;ylgnbu&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/palette/ylorrd">palette / ylorrd</a> <span class="about">ColorBrewer&#039;s multi-hue sequential scale YlOrRd of nine classes, from a pale yellow through orange and red to a dark crimson (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), for heat, risk or intensity: its hue turns as its lightness falls, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes.</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-brc">{</span><span class="hl-tag">pad:palette</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;ylorrd&#039;</span><span class="hl-brc">/}</span></code></pre>
    </div>
    
  </div>
</section>

    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/recurrence/rrule">recurrence / rrule</a> <span class="about"># Calendar recurrence expansion</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-fn">padOccurrences</span> <span class="hl-pun">(</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;rrule&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-pun">[</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;start&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">=&gt;</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2026-11-02&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;rrule&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">=&gt;</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;FREQ=WEEKLY;COUNT=4&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">]</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2026-11-01&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2031-12-01&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">)</span><span class="hl-pun">;</span></code></pre>

    <div class="cheat-item">
      <a class="name" href="?kind/recurrence/weekly">recurrence / weekly</a> <span class="about"># Calendar recurrence expansion</span>
      <pre class="code"><code><span class="hl-fn">padOccurrences</span> <span class="hl-pun">(</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;weekly&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-pun">[</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;start&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">=&gt;</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2026-11-02&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;count&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">=&gt;</span> <span class="hl-num">4</span> <span class="hl-pun">]</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2026-11-01&#039;</span><span class="hl-pun">,</span> <span class="hl-str">&#039;2031-12-01&#039;</span> <span class="hl-pun">)</span><span class="hl-pun">;</span></code></pre>
    </div>
